[ℹ️] [2026-01-03 11:49:54 UTC] [Agent: Scribe] [Project: scribe_tool_output_refinement] Tool call: read_file | format_requested=readable; response_data={"chunks": [{"byte_end": 11567, "byte_start": 0, "chunk_index": 0, "content": "\n# \ud83d\udd2c Research: Reminder System Architecture Analysis \u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\n**Author:** ResearchAgent\n**Version:** v1.0\n**Status:** Complete\n**Last Updated:** 2026-01-03 11:44:30 UTC\n\n> This document provides comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system architecture to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign. It examines current file-based cooldown mechanisms, session tracking capabilities, database infrastructure readiness, and tool integration patterns to identify gaps and design opportunities for session-isolated reminder deduplication.\n\n---\n## Executive Summary\n<!-- ID: executive_summary -->\n**Primary Objective:** Conduct comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign that enables proper session isolation and failure-triggered reminder prioritization.\n\n**Key Takeaways:**\n- **Critical Gap Identified:** Current file-based cooldown cache (`data/reminder_cooldowns.json`) lacks session isolation - reminders persist across all sessions for the same project+agent+tool combination\n- **Infrastructure Ready:** Robust session tracking already exists in DB (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables) with session_id support in `execution_context.py`\n- **Novel Enhancement:** No prior session-aware caching work exists across Scribe projects - this will be pioneering implementation\n- **Missing Table:** `reminder_history` table needs to be added to SQLite schema for DB-integrated cooldown tracking\n- **Tool Integration Gap:** No distinction between success/failure context when generating reminders - all reminders treated equally\n- **Singleton Pattern:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance shared across all sessions prevents per-session state management\n\n\n---\n## Research Scope\n<!-- ID: research_scope -->\n**Research Lead:** ResearchAgent\n\n**Investigation Window:** 2026-01-03 11:40 \u2014 2026-01-03 11:44 UTC\n\n**Focus Areas:**\n- \u2705 Current reminder architecture (`utils/reminder_engine.py`)\n- \u2705 Cooldown cache persistence mechanism (file-based JSON)\n- \u2705 Session tracking infrastructure (`shared/execution_context.py`)\n- \u2705 Database schema analysis (`storage/sqlite.py`)\n- \u2705 Tool integration patterns (append_entry, read_file)\n- \u2705 Cross-project learning (session-aware caching patterns)\n\n**Dependencies & Constraints:**\n- **Existing Infrastructure:** Must integrate with existing session tracking (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables)\n- **Backwards Compatibility:** File-based cooldown cache currently contains 47KB of data from test runs and production usage\n- **Singleton Architecture:** Current global singleton pattern conflicts with per-session state requirements\n- **10-Minute Context Window:** Design must support detection and reset of 10-minute context boundaries\n\n\n---\n## Findings\n<!-- ID: findings -->\n\n### Finding 1: File-Based Cooldown Mechanism Lacks Session Isolation\n- **Summary:** Current implementation uses composite hash keys (`project_root agent_id tool_name reminder_key`) that persist across all sessions, preventing proper session-aware cooldown isolation\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:282-287` - `_get_reminder_hash()` constructs keys without session_id component\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:236-255` - `_save_cooldown_cache()` persists to `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` with atomic write pattern\n  - `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` - Real-world file contains 47KB of entries with format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}: {timestamp}`\n- **Confidence:** 0.97 (High)\n\n### Finding 2: Robust Session Tracking Infrastructure Already Exists\n- **Summary:** Database contains comprehensive session tracking tables (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`) with TTL, identity_key, and transport_session_id mapping capabilities\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:681-692` - `agent_sessions` table schema with identity_key, TTL, expires_at fields\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:740-748` - `scribe_sessions` table with transport_session_id \u2192 stable session_id mapping\n  - `shared/execution_context.py:62-112` - `get_or_create_session_id()` implements 3-tier lookup: memory cache \u2192 DB \u2192 create new\n- **Confidence:** 0.96 (High)\n\n### Finding 3: Missing reminder_history Table in Database\n- **Summary:** No `reminder_history` table exists in current SQLite schema - all reminder cooldown tracking is file-based only\n- **Evidence:**\n  - Searched `storage/sqlite.py` for `CREATE TABLE` statements - found `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`, `agent_projects`, but no reminder-related tables\n  - Grep for `reminder` in CREATE TABLE context returned no matches\n- **Confidence:** 1.0 (Absolute)\n\n### Finding 4: Singleton Pattern Prevents Per-Session State\n- **Summary:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance in `reminders.py` is shared across all sessions, preventing session-specific cooldown state management\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `reminders.py:20-28` - `_get_engine()` returns global singleton instance\n  - `reminder_engine.py:66-87` - `ReminderEngine.__init__()` initializes single `ReminderHistory` object for all sessions\n- **Confidence:** 0.93 (High)\n\n### Finding 5: No Success/Failure Context in Reminder Generation\n- **Summary:** Tools call `get_reminders()` after main execution completes, but no context is passed about operation outcome (success vs failure) to enable failure-triggered priority reminders\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `tools/append_entry.py:1210-1212` - `get_reminders()` called after entry writing completes\n  - `tools/read_file.py:502-517` - Reminders generated regardless of read operation outcome\n  - No status/success parameter passed to `get_reminders()` function\n- **Confidence:** 0.91 (High)\n\n### Finding 6: Session Age Tracking Exists But Is Unused\n- **Summary:** Session age is calculated from `state.session_started_at` but not integrated into cooldown hash or reset logic\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `reminders.py:179-186` - `session_age_minutes` extracted from state in `_build_legacy_context()`\n  - `reminder_engine.py:54` - `ReminderContext` includes `session_age_minutes` field\n  - No usage of `session_age_minutes` in cooldown reset or hash generation logic\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (High)\n\n### Finding 7: Cross-Project Search Revealed No Prior Work\n- **Summary:** No existing implementations of session-aware cooldown caching exist across all Scribe projects - this redesign will be pioneering work\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='session cooldown cache isolation')` - No results\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='reminder deduplication session-aware')` - No results\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (Medium-High - absence of evidence)\n\n\n---\n## Technical Analysis\n<!-- ID: technical_analysis -->\n\n### Code Patterns Identified\n\n**Current Architecture:**\n```\n\u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n\u2502 Tool Execution (append_entry, read_file, etc.)             \u2502\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500> get_reminders(project, tool_name, state)             \u2502\n\u2502        \u2514\u2500> _build_legacy_context()                         \u2502\n\u2502             \u2514\u2500> ReminderEngine.generate_reminders()        \u2502\n\u2502                  \u2514\u2500> _get_reminder_hash()                  \u2502\n\u2502                       \u2514\u2500> f\"{project_root} {agent_id}      \u2502\n\u2502                             {tool_name} {reminder_key}\"    \u2502\n\u2502                            (NO SESSION_ID)                 \u2502\n\u2502   Cooldown Cache: data/reminder_cooldowns.json            \u2502\n\u2502   Session Tracking: execution_context.py (UNUSED)         \u2502\n\u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n```\n\n**Hash Generation Pattern (Current):**\n- Format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\n- Persisted to: `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\n- Lifetime: 24 hours (configurable via `cooldown_cache_retention_minutes`)\n- Atomic writes: Uses `.tmp` file then `replace()` for safety\n\n**Session Tracking Pattern (Existing but Unused):**\n- `ExecutionContext.session_id` - Stable UUID persisted across restarts\n- `ExecutionContext.transport_session_id` - Unstable ID from MCP transport\n- `RouterContextManager.get_or_create_session_id()` - 3-tier lookup with DB persistence\n- Tables: `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`\n\n### System Interactions\n\n**Current Dependencies:**\n1. **Reminder Engine \u2192 File System**\n   - Reads/writes `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\n   - Atomic write pattern with `.tmp` file\n   - 24-hour cleanup via `_cleanup_cooldown_cache()`\n\n2. **Execution Context \u2192 Database**\n   - `upsert_session()` persists session to `scribe_sessions` table\n   - `get_session_by_transport()` retrieves stable session_id from transport_session_id\n   - Session heartbeat updates `last_active_at` timestamp\n\n3. **Tool Layer \u2192 Reminder Engine**\n   - Post-execution call to `get_reminders()`\n   - No feedback about operation success/failure\n   - State object passed contains `session_started_at` but no session_id\n\n**Missing Integration:**\n- Reminder engine does NOT query database for session info\n- Session_id NOT included in cooldown hash keys\n- No link between `reminder_history` and `agent_sessions` tables\n\n### Risk Assessment\n\n**Technical Risks:**\n- \u2705 **File-Based Race Conditions:** Atomic write pattern (`.tmp` + `replace()`) mitigates concurrent write risks\n- \u26a0\ufe0f **Singleton Shared State:** Global `_reminder_engine` means concurrent sessions share cooldown state (HIGH RISK)\n- \u26a0\ufe0f **Test Data Pollution:** `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` contains test project UUIDs mixed with production data (MEDIUM RISK)\n- \u2705 **Database Session Infrastructure:** Existing tables and methods are production-ready (LOW RISK)\n- \u26a0\ufe0f **Migration Complexity:** Moving from file-based to DB-based cooldowns requires careful data migration (MEDIUM RISK)\n\n\n---\n## Recommendations\n<!-- ID: recommendations -->\n\n### Immediate Next Steps\n\n**Architecture Phase:**\n1. **Design `reminder_history` Table Schema**\n   - Columns: `id`, `session_id`, `project_root`, `agent_id`, `tool_name`, `reminder_key`, `shown_at`, `context` (success/failure)\n   - Indexes: `(session_id, reminder_key)`, `(shown_at)` for TTL cleanup\n   - Foreign key: `session_id` \u2192 `scribe_sessions.session_id` (ON DELETE CASCADE)\n\n2. **Define Session Reset Strategy**\n   - **Option A: Time-Based (10-minute idle)** - Use `agent_sessions.last_active_at` to detect context boundaries\n   - **Option B: Context-Based** - Explicit session end events trigger cooldown reset\n   - **Recommendation:** Hybrid approach - reset on 10-minute idle OR explicit session end\n\n3. **Specify Failure-Triggered Reminder Priority**\n   - Add `operation_status` parameter to `get_reminders()` function\n   - Prioritize failure-context reminders (e.g., \"no logs\" after failed `append_entry`)\n   - Lower priority for success-context reminders\n\n**Implementation Phase:**\n4. **Refactor Hash Generation**\n   - Include `session_id` in cooldown hash: `{session_id} {project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\n   - Update `_get_reminder_hash()` to accept `session_id` parameter\n\n5. **Create DB Storage Methods**\n   - `upsert_reminder_shown(session_id, reminder_key, context)` - Record reminder display\n   - `check_reminder_cooldown(session_id, reminder_key, cooldown_minutes)` - Query recent displays\n", "line_end": 200, "line_start": 1}], "frontmatter": {}, "frontmatter_byte_count": 0, Problem Statement\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\n## 1. Problem Statement\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\n- **Context:** Scribe MCP tools return JSON-wrapped outputs that are difficult for AI agents to read and use effectively. The `read_file` tool exemplifies this: content is buried in nested structures with escaped newlines, no line numbers, and metadata mixed with content. Agents avoid using these tools despite their audit capabilities.\n- **Goals:**\n  - Make ALL Scribe MCP tool outputs agent-friendly and highly readable\n  - Preserve full audit trail (sha256, provenance, timestamps, agent identity)\n  - Start with `read_file` as the priority tool, then expand to all tools\n  - Create output formats that agents WANT to use\n  - Maintain backward compatibility for existing integrations\n- **Non-Goals:**\n  - Breaking existing API contracts\n  - Removing audit/provenance capabilities\n  - Creating entirely new tools (we refine existing ones)\n- **Success Metrics:**\n  - Agents prefer Scribe tools over raw file operations\n  - Output is readable at a glance (line numbers, actual line breaks)\n  - Audit data is accessible but not obstructive\n  - All existing tests continue to pass\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\n## 2. Requirements & Constraints\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\n\n### Functional Requirements\n\n**FR1: Multi-Format Output Support**\n- All tools MUST support three output formats: `readable`, `structured`, `compact`\n- Format selection via optional `format` parameter (default: `structured` for backward compatibility)\n- Each format serves distinct use case:\n  - `readable`: Agent-friendly with line numbers, visual separators, actual line breaks\n  - `structured`: Current JSON format for programmatic consumers (backward compatible)\n  - `compact`: Minimal output for token-constrained scenarios\n\n**FR2: Readable Format Specification**\n- Content MUST be line-numbered using cat -n style: `  1\u2192content`, `  2\u2192content`\n- Line numbers right-aligned with arrow separator for scannability\n- Actual line breaks (NOT escaped `\\n`)\n- Metadata in separate collapsible section with visual boundaries\n- ASCII art separators for clear visual hierarchy\n\n**FR3: Priority Tool Coverage**\n1. `read_file` - File content with provenance (HIGHEST PRIORITY)\n2. `append_entry` - Log confirmation\n3. `read_recent` - Recent log entries\n4. `query_entries` - Search results\n5. `list_projects` - Project listing\n6. All remaining 9 tools\n\n**FR4: Metadata Preservation**\n- All audit data MUST be preserved: sha256, timestamps, provenance, agent identity\n- Metadata placed in separate section (not mixed with content)\n- Collapsible metadata blocks using ASCII separators\n- Full audit trail accessible in all formats\n\n### Non-Functional Requirements\n\n**NFR1: Backward Compatibility**\n- Default format MUST be `structured` (current behavior)\n- Existing tool responses MUST remain unchanged when format parameter not specified\n- All existing tests MUST pass without modification\n- No breaking changes to tool APIs\n\n**NFR2: Performance**\n- Output formatting MUST NOT degrade performance below existing baselines\n- `readable` format generation \u2264 5ms overhead per tool call\n- Memory overhead \u2264 2x current usage (for formatted strings)\n- Existing performance test suite MUST pass\n\n**NFR3: Maintainability**\n- Extend existing `utils/response.py` - NO new modules\n- Single source of truth for formatting logic\n- Consistent API across all tools\n- Clear documentation for format implementations\n\n", "line_end": 100, "line_start": 1}, "frontmatter": {"category": "engineering", "created_by": "Corta Labs", "doc_type": "architecture", "id": "scribe_tool_output_refinement-architecture", "last_updated": "2026-01-02", "maintained_by": "Corta Labs", "owners": [], "related_docs": [], "status": "draft", "summary": "", "tags": [], "title": "\ud83c\udfd7\ufe0f Architecture Guide \u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement", "version": "0.1"}, "frontmatter_byte_count": 336, "frontmatter_line_count": 15, "frontmatter_raw": "---\nid: scribe_tool_output_refinement-architecture\ntitle: \"\\U0001F3D7\\uFE0F Architecture Guide \\u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\"\ndoc_type: architecture\ncategory: engineering\nstatus: draft\nversion: '0.1'\nlast_updated: '2026-01-02'\nmaintained_by: Corta Labs\ncreated_by: Corta Labs\nowners: []\nrelated_docs: []\ntags: []\nsummary: ''\n---\n", "has_frontmatter": true, "mode": "line_range", "ok": true, "reminders": [{"category": "context", "context": "Entries:    Last: ", "emoji": "\ud83c\udfaf", "level": "info", "message": "Project: scribe_tool_output_refinement", "score": 3, "tone": "neutral"}], "scan": {"absolute_path": "/home/austin/projects/MCP_SPINE/scribe_mcp/.scribe/docs/dev_plans/scribe_tool_output_refinement/ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.md", "byte_size": 49700, "encoding": "utf-8", "estimated_chunk_count": 7, "line_count": 1274, "newline_type": "LF", "repo_relative_path": ".scribe/docs/dev_plans/scribe_tool_output_refinement/ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.md", "sha256": "355aacd68105324355dbbc9ed15fcb4bac45638efc91ea679cf8f0996cbcef92"}}; timestamp=2026-01-03T11:50:12.538868Z; tool=read_file; priority=medium; log_type=tool_logs; content_type=log

In-memory cache (fast path)\n        2. Database lookup (persistence layer)\n        3. Create new session and persist\n\n        Args:\n            transport_session_id: Unstable ID from transport layer\n\n        Returns:\n            Stable session UUID that persists across restarts\n        \"\"\"\n        if not transport_session_id:\n            raise ValueError(\"ExecutionContext requires transport_session_id\")\n\n        async with self._lock:\n            # TIER 1: Check in-memory cache (fast path)\n            existing = self._transport_sessions.get(transport_session_id)\n            if existing:\n                return existing\n\n            # TIER 2: Check database for existing session (persistence layer)\n            if self._storage_backend and hasattr(self._storage_backend, \"get_session_by_transport\"):\n                # NO SILENT ERRORS - let it fail loudly so we can see what's broken\n                db_session = await self._storage_backend.get_session_by_transport(transport_session_id)\n                if db_session and db_session.get(\"session_id\"):\n                    session_id = db_session[\"session_id\"]\n                    # Cache it for future requests (performance optimization)\n                    self._transport_sessions[transport_session_id] = session_id\n                    return session_id\n\n            # TIER 3: Create new session (not found in cache or DB)\n            session_id = str(uuid.uuid4())\n\n            # Cache immediately\n            self._transport_sessions[transport_session_id] = session_id\n\n            # TIER 3b: Persist to database immediately\n            if self._storage_backend and hasattr(self._storage_backend, \"upsert_session\"):\n                # NO SILENT ERRORS - let it fail loudly so we can see what's broken\n                await self._storage_backend.upsert_session(\n                    session_id=session_id,\n                    transport_session_id=transport_session_id,\n                    repo_root=None,  # Will be set later by set_project\n                    mode=\"sentinel\",  # Default mode\n                )\n\n            return session_id\n\n    def _build_agent_identity(self, payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> AgentIdentity:\n        agent_kind = os.environ.get(\"SCRIBE_AGENT_KIND\", \"other\")\n", "line_end": 115, "line_start": 60}, "frontmatter": {}, "frontmatter_byte_count": 0, "frontmatter_line_count": 0, "frontmatter_raw": "", "has_frontmatter": false, "mode": "line_range", "ok": true, "reminders": [{"category": "context", "context": "Entries:    Last: ", "emoji": "\ud83c\udfaf", "level": "info", "message": "Project: scribe_tool_output_refinement", "score": 3, "tone": "neutral"}], "scan": {"absolute_path": "/home/austin/projects/MCP_SPINE/scribe_mcp/shared/execution_context.py", "byte_size": 7745, "encoding": "utf-8", "estimated_chunk_count": 1, "line_count": 194, "newline_type": "LF", "repo_relative_path": "shared/execution_context.py", "sha256": "12d7aee9a33a1ffc5cf0c1572e392d74389e6d42fd76eb920759667b01f8f465"}}; timestamp=2026-01-03T11:50:32.746036Z; tool=read_file; priority=medium; log_type=tool_logs; content_type=log

[ℹ️] [2026-01-03 11:51:19 UTC] [Agent: Scribe] [Project: scribe_tool_output_refinement] Tool call: read_file | format_requested=readable; response_data={"chunk": {"byte_end": 1893, "byte_start": 565, "content": "## 1. Problem Statement\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\n## 1. Problem Statement\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\n- **Context:** Scribe MCP tools return JSON-wrapped outputs that are difficult for AI agents to read and use effectively. The `read_file` tool exemplifies this: content is buried in nested structures with escaped newlines, no line numbers, and metadata mixed with content. Agents avoid using these tools despite their audit capabilities.\n- **Goals:**\n  - Make ALL Scribe MCP tool outputs agent-friendly and highly readable\n  - Preserve full audit trail (sha256, provenance, timestamps, agent identity)\n  - Start with `read_file` as the priority tool, then expand to all tools\n  - Create output formats that agents WANT to use\n  - Maintain backward compatibility for existing integrations\n- **Non-Goals:**\n  - Breaking existing API contracts\n  - Removing audit/provenance capabilities\n  - Creating entirely new tools (we refine existing ones)\n- **Success Metrics:**\n  - Agents prefer Scribe tools over raw file operations\n  - Output is readable at a glance (line numbers, actual line breaks)\n  - Audit data is accessible but not obstructive\n  - All existing tests continue to pass\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\n## 2. Requirements & Constraints\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\n\n### Functional Requirements\n", "line_end": 50, "line_start": 26}, "frontmatter": {"category": "engineering", "created_by": "Corta Labs", "doc_type": "architecture", "id": "scribe_tool_output_refinement-architecture", "last_updated": "2026-01-02", "maintained_by": "Corta Labs", "owners": [], "related_docs": [], "status": "draft", "summary": "", "tags": [], "title": "\ud83c\udfd7\ufe0f Architecture Guide \u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement", "version": "0.1"}, "frontmatter_byte_count": 336, "frontmatter_line_count": 15, "frontmatter_raw": "---\nid: scribe_tool_output_refinement-architecture\ntitle: \"\\U0001F3D7\\uFE0F Architecture Guide \\u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\"\ndoc_type: architecture\ncategory: engineering\nstatus: draft\nversion: '0.1'\nlast_updated: '2026-01-02'\nmaintained_by: Corta Labs\ncreated_by: Corta Labs\nowners: []\nrelated_docs: []\ntags: []\nsummary: ''\n---\n", "has_frontmatter": true, "mode": "line_range", "ok": true, "reminders": [{"category": "context", "context": "Entries:    Last: ", "emoji": "\ud83c\udfaf", "level": "info", "message": "Project: scribe_tool_output_refinement", "score": 3, "tone": "neutral"}], "scan": {"absolute_path": "/home/austin/projects/MCP_SPINE/scribe_mcp/.scribe/docs/dev_plans/scribe_tool_output_refinement/ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.md", "byte_size": 49700, "encoding": "utf-8", "estimated_chunk_count": 7, "line_count": 1274, "newline_type": "LF", "repo_relative_path": ".scribe/docs/dev_plans/scribe_tool_output_refinement/ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.md", "sha256": "355aacd68105324355dbbc9ed15fcb4bac45638efc91ea679cf8f0996cbcef92"}}; timestamp=2026-01-03T11:51:19.742848Z; tool=read_file; priority=medium; log_type=tool_logs; content_type=log

48851, "content": "    \"required_meta\": [\"tool\", \"format_requested\"],\n    \"rotation_threshold\": 1000,\n    \"description\": \"Structured JSON audit trail for all tool calls\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Benefits of This Approach\n\n1. **Agent UX**: Readable by default - agents see clean output immediately\n2. **Complete Audit**: JSON logged regardless of display format\n3. **Queryable**: `tool_logs` can be searched/analyzed\n4. **Flexible**: Agents can still request JSON when needed\n5. **No Data Loss**: Even readable responses have full JSON backing\n\n### Implementation Impact\n\n**Phase 0 additions**:\n- Add `tool_logs` log type to config\n- `finalize_tool_response()` always logs to tool_logs first\n- JSONL format for efficient append/query\n\n**All phases**: \n- Change default from `format=\"structured\"` to `format=\"readable\"`\n- Ensure tool_logs capture happens before format conversion\n", "line_end": 1274, "line_start": 1250}, [ℹ️] [2026-01-03 12:01:25 UTC] [Agent: Scribe] [Project: scribe_tool_output_refinement] Tool call: read_file | format_requested=readable; response_data={"chunks": [{"byte_end": 11567, "byte_start": 0, "chunk_index": 0, "content": "\n# \ud83d\udd2c Research: Reminder System Architecture Analysis \u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\n**Author:** ResearchAgent\n**Version:** v1.0\n**Status:** Complete\n**Last Updated:** 2026-01-03 11:44:30 UTC\n\n> This document provides comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system architecture to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign. It examines current file-based cooldown mechanisms, session tracking capabilities, database infrastructure readiness, and tool integration patterns to identify gaps and design opportunities for session-isolated reminder deduplication.\n\n---\n## Executive Summary\n<!-- ID: executive_summary -->\n**Primary Objective:** Conduct comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign that enables proper session isolation and failure-triggered reminder prioritization.\n\n**Key Takeaways:**\n- **Critical Gap Identified:** Current file-based cooldown cache (`data/reminder_cooldowns.json`) lacks session isolation - reminders persist across all sessions for the same project+agent+tool combination\n- **Infrastructure Ready:** Robust session tracking already exists in DB (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables) with session_id support in `execution_context.py`\n- **Novel Enhancement:** No prior session-aware caching work exists across Scribe projects - this will be pioneering implementation\n- **Missing Table:** `reminder_history` table needs to be added to SQLite schema for DB-integrated cooldown tracking\n- **Tool Integration Gap:** No distinction between success/failure context when generating reminders - all reminders treated equally\n- **Singleton Pattern:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance shared across all sessions prevents per-session state management\n\n\n---\n## Research Scope\n<!-- ID: research_scope -->\n**Research Lead:** ResearchAgent\n\n**Investigation Window:** 2026-01-03 11:40 \u2014 2026-01-03 11:44 UTC\n\n**Focus Areas:**\n- \u2705 Current reminder architecture (`utils/reminder_engine.py`)\n- \u2705 Cooldown cache persistence mechanism (file-based JSON)\n- \u2705 Session tracking infrastructure (`shared/execution_context.py`)\n- \u2705 Database schema analysis (`storage/sqlite.py`)\n- \u2705 Tool integration patterns (append_entry, read_file)\n- \u2705 Cross-project learning (session-aware caching patterns)\n\n**Dependencies & Constraints:**\n- **Existing Infrastructure:** Must integrate with existing session tracking (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables)\n- **Backwards Compatibility:** File-based cooldown cache currently contains 47KB of data from test runs and production usage\n- **Singleton Architecture:** Current global singleton pattern conflicts with per-session state requirements\n- **10-Minute Context Window:** Design must support detection and reset of 10-minute context boundaries\n\n\n---\n## Findings\n<!-- ID: findings -->\n\n### Finding 1: File-Based Cooldown Mechanism Lacks Session Isolation\n- **Summary:** Current implementation uses composite hash keys (`project_root agent_id tool_name reminder_key`) that persist across all sessions, preventing proper session-aware cooldown isolation\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:282-287` - `_get_reminder_hash()` constructs keys without session_id component\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:236-255` - `_save_cooldown_cache()` persists to `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` with atomic write pattern\n  - `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` - Real-world file contains 47KB of entries with format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}: {timestamp}`\n- **Confidence:** 0.97 (High)\n\n### Finding 2: Robust Session Tracking Infrastructure Already Exists\n- **Summary:** Database contains comprehensive session tracking tables (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`) with TTL, identity_key, and transport_session_id mapping capabilities\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:681-692` - `agent_sessions` table schema with identity_key, TTL, expires_at fields\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:740-748` - `scribe_sessions` table with transport_session_id \u2192 stable session_id mapping\n  - `shared/execution_context.py:62-112` - `get_or_create_session_id()` implements 3-tier lookup: memory cache \u2192 DB \u2192 create new\n- **Confidence:** 0.96 (High)\n\n### Finding 3: Missing reminder_history Table in Database\n- **Summary:** No `reminder_history` table exists in current SQLite schema - all reminder cooldown tracking is file-based only\n- **Evidence:**\n  - Searched `storage/sqlite.py` for `CREATE TABLE` statements - found `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`, `agent_projects`, but no reminder-related tables\n  - Grep for `reminder` in CREATE TABLE context returned no matches\n- **Confidence:** 1.0 (Absolute)\n\n### Finding 4: Singleton Pattern Prevents Per-Session State\n- **Summary:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance in `reminders.py` is shared across all sessions, preventing session-specific cooldown state management\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `reminders.py:20-28` - `_get_engine()` returns global singleton instance\n  - `reminder_engine.py:66-87` - `ReminderEngine.__init__()` initializes single `ReminderHistory` object for all sessions\n- **Confidence:** 0.93 (High)\n\n### Finding 5: No Success/Failure Context in Reminder Generation\n- **Summary:** Tools call `get_reminders()` after main execution completes, but no context is passed about operation outcome (success vs failure) to enable failure-triggered priority reminders\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `tools/append_entry.py:1210-1212` - `get_reminders()` called after entry writing completes\n  - `tools/read_file.py:502-517` - Reminders generated regardless of read operation outcome\n  - No status/success parameter passed to `get_reminders()` function\n- **Confidence:** 0.91 (High)\n\n### Finding 6: Session Age Tracking Exists But Is Unused\n- **Summary:** Session age is calculated from `state.session_started_at` but not integrated into cooldown hash or reset logic\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `reminders.py:179-186` - `session_age_minutes` extracted from state in `_build_legacy_context()`\n  - `reminder_engine.py:54` - `ReminderContext` includes `session_age_minutes` field\n  - No usage of `session_age_minutes` in cooldown reset or hash generation logic\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (High)\n\n### Finding 7: Cross-Project Search Revealed No Prior Work\n- **Summary:** No existing implementations of session-aware cooldown caching exist across all Scribe projects - this redesign will be pioneering work\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='session cooldown cache isolation')` - No results\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='reminder deduplication session-aware')` - No results\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (Medium-High - absence of evidence)\n\n\n---\n## Technical Analysis\n<!-- ID: technical_analysis -->\n\n### Code Patterns Identified\n\n**Current Architecture:**\n```\n\u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n\u2502 Tool Execution (append_entry, read_file, etc.)             \u2502\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500> get_reminders(project, tool_name, state)             \u2502\n\u2502        \u2514\u2500> _build_legacy_context()                         \u2502\n\u2502             \u2514\u2500> ReminderEngine.generate_reminders()        \u2502\n\u2502                  \u2514\u2500> _get_reminder_hash()                  \u2502\n\u2502                       \u2514\u2500> f\"{project_root} {agent_id}      \u2502\n\u2502                             {tool_name} {reminder_key}\"    \u2502\n\u2502                            (NO SESSION_ID)                 \u2502\n\u2502   Cooldown Cache: data/reminder_cooldowns.json            \u2502\n\u2502   Session Tracking: execution_context.py (UNUSED)         \u2502\n\u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n```\n\n**Hash Generation Pattern (Current):**\n- Format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\n- Persisted to: `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\n- Lifetime: 24 hours (configurable via `cooldown_cache_retention_minutes`)\n- Atomic writes: Uses `.tmp` file then `replace()` for safety\n\n**Session Tracking Pattern (Existing but Unused):**\n- `ExecutionContext.session_id` - Stable UUID persisted across restarts\n- `ExecutionContext.transport_session_id` - Unstable ID from MCP transport\n- `RouterContextManager.get_or_create_session_id()` - 3-tier lookup with DB persistence\n- Tables: `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`\n\n### System Interactions\n\n**Current Dependencies:**\n1. **Reminder Engine \u2192 File System**\n   - Reads/writes `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\n   - Atomic write pattern with `.tmp` file\n   - 24-hour cleanup via `_cleanup_cooldown_cache()`\n\n2. **Execution Context \u2192 Database**\n   - `upsert_session()` persists session to `scribe_sessions` table\n   - `get_session_by_transport()` retrieves stable session_id from transport_session_id\n   - Session heartbeat updates `last_active_at` timestamp\n\n3. **Tool Layer \u2192 Reminder Engine**\n   - Post-execution call to `get_reminders()`\n   - No feedback about operation success/failure\n   - State object passed contains `session_started_at` but no session_id\n\n**Missing Integration:**\n- Reminder engine does NOT query database for session info\n- Session_id NOT included in cooldown hash keys\n- No link between `reminder_history` and `agent_sessions` tables\n\n### Risk Assessment\n\n**Technical Risks:**\n- \u2705 **File-Based Race Conditions:** Atomic write pattern (`.tmp` + `replace()`) mitigates concurrent write risks\n- \u26a0\ufe0f **Singleton Shared State:** Global `_reminder_engine` means concurrent sessions share cooldown state (HIGH RISK)\n- \u26a0\ufe0f **Test Data Pollution:** `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` contains test project UUIDs mixed with production data (MEDIUM RISK)\n- \u2705 **Database Session Infrastructure:** Existing tables and methods are production-ready (LOW RISK)\n- \u26a0\ufe0f **Migration Complexity:** Moving from file-based to DB-based cooldowns requires careful data migration (MEDIUM RISK)\n\n\n---\n## Recommendations\n<!-- ID: recommendations -->\n\n### Immediate Next Steps\n\n**Architecture Phase:**\n1. **Design `reminder_history` Table Schema**\n   - Columns: `id`, `session_id`, `project_root`, `agent_id`, `tool_name`, `reminder_key`, `shown_at`, `context` (success/failure)\n   - Indexes: `(session_id, reminder_key)`, `(shown_at)` for TTL cleanup\n   - Foreign key: `session_id` \u2192 `scribe_sessions.session_id` (ON DELETE CASCADE)\n\n2. **Define Session Reset Strategy**\n   - **Option A: Time-Based (10-minute idle)** - Use `agent_sessions.last_active_at` to detect context boundaries\n   - **Option B: Context-Based** - Explicit session end events trigger cooldown reset\n   - **Recommendation:** Hybrid approach - reset on 10-minute idle OR explicit session end\n\n3. **Specify Failure-Triggered Reminder Priority**\n   - Add `operation_status` parameter to `get_reminders()` function\n   - Prioritize failure-context reminders (e.g., \"no logs\" after failed `append_entry`)\n   - Lower priority for success-context reminders\n\n**Implementation Phase:**\n4. **Refactor Hash Generation**\n   - Include `session_id` in cooldown hash: `{session_id} {project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\n   - Update `_get_reminder_hash()` to accept `session_id` parameter\n\n5. **Create DB Storage Methods**\n   - `upsert_reminder_shown(session_id, reminder_key, context)` - Record reminder display\n   - `check_reminder_cooldown(session_id, reminder_key, cooldown_minutes)` - Query recent displays\n", "line_end": 200, "line_start": 1}], "frontmatter": {}, "frontmatter_byte_count": 0, It examines current file-based cooldown mechanisms, session tracking capabilities, database infrastructure readiness, and tool integration patterns to identify gaps and design opportunities for session-isolated reminder deduplication.\\n\\n---\\n## Executive Summary\\n<!-- ID: executive_summary -->\\n**Primary Objective:** Conduct comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign that enables proper session isolation and failure-triggered reminder prioritization.\\n\\n**Key Takeaways:**\\n- **Critical Gap Identified:** Current file-based cooldown cache (`data/reminder_cooldowns.json`) lacks session isolation - reminders persist across all sessions for the same project+agent+tool combination\\n- **Infrastructure Ready:** Robust session tracking already exists in DB (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables) with session_id support in `execution_context.py`\\n- **Novel Enhancement:** No prior session-aware caching work exists across Scribe projects - this will be pioneering implementation\\n- **Missing Table:** `reminder_history` table needs to be added to SQLite schema for DB-integrated cooldown tracking\\n- **Tool Integration Gap:** No distinction between success/failure context when generating reminders - all reminders treated equally\\n- **Singleton Pattern:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance shared across all sessions prevents per-session state management\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Research Scope\\n<!-- ID: research_scope -->\\n**Research Lead:** ResearchAgent\\n\\n**Investigation Window:** 2026-01-03 11:40 \\u2014 2026-01-03 11:44 UTC\\n\\n**Focus Areas:**\\n- \\u2705 Current reminder architecture (`utils/reminder_engine.py`)\\n- \\u2705 Cooldown cache persistence mechanism (file-based JSON)\\n- \\u2705 Session tracking infrastructure (`shared/execution_context.py`)\\n- \\u2705 Database schema analysis (`storage/sqlite.py`)\\n- \\u2705 Tool integration patterns (append_entry, read_file)\\n- \\u2705 Cross-project learning (session-aware caching patterns)\\n\\n**Dependencies & Constraints:**\\n- **Existing Infrastructure:** Must integrate with existing session tracking (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables)\\n- **Backwards Compatibility:** File-based cooldown cache currently contains 47KB of data from test runs and production usage\\n- **Singleton Architecture:** Current global singleton pattern conflicts with per-session state requirements\\n- **10-Minute Context Window:** Design must support detection and reset of 10-minute context boundaries\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Findings\\n<!-- ID: findings -->\\n\\n### Finding 1: File-Based Cooldown Mechanism Lacks Session Isolation\\n- **Summary:** Current implementation uses composite hash keys (`project_root agent_id tool_name reminder_key`) that persist across all sessions, preventing proper session-aware cooldown isolation\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:282-287` - `_get_reminder_hash()` constructs keys without session_id component\\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:236-255` - `_save_cooldown_cache()` persists to `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` with atomic write pattern\\n  - `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` - Real-world file contains 47KB of entries with format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}: {timestamp}`\\n- **Confidence:** 0.97 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 2: Robust Session Tracking Infrastructure Already Exists\\n- **Summary:** Database contains comprehensive session tracking tables (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`) with TTL, identity_key, and transport_session_id mapping capabilities\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:681-692` - `agent_sessions` table schema with identity_key, TTL, expires_at fields\\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:740-748` - `scribe_sessions` table with transport_session_id \\u2192 stable session_id mapping\\n  - `shared/execution_context.py:62-112` - `get_or_create_session_id()` implements 3-tier lookup: memory cache \\u2192 DB \\u2192 create new\\n- **Confidence:** 0.96 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 3: Missing reminder_history Table in Database\\n- **Summary:** No `reminder_history` table exists in current SQLite schema - all reminder cooldown tracking is file-based only\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - Searched `storage/sqlite.py` for `CREATE TABLE` statements - found `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`, `agent_projects`, but no reminder-related tables\\n  - Grep for `reminder` in CREATE TABLE context returned no matches\\n- **Confidence:** 1.0 (Absolute)\\n\\n### Finding 4: Singleton Pattern Prevents Per-Session State\\n- **Summary:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance in `reminders.py` is shared across all sessions, preventing session-specific cooldown state management\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `reminders.py:20-28` - `_get_engine()` returns global singleton instance\\n  - `reminder_engine.py:66-87` - `ReminderEngine.__init__()` initializes single `ReminderHistory` object for all sessions\\n- **Confidence:** 0.93 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 5: No Success/Failure Context in Reminder Generation\\n- **Summary:** Tools call `get_reminders()` after main execution completes, but no context is passed about operation outcome (success vs failure) to enable failure-triggered priority reminders\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `tools/append_entry.py:1210-1212` - `get_reminders()` called after entry writing completes\\n  - `tools/read_file.py:502-517` - Reminders generated regardless of read operation outcome\\n  - No status/success parameter passed to `get_reminders()` function\\n- **Confidence:** 0.91 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 6: Session Age Tracking Exists But Is Unused\\n- **Summary:** Session age is calculated from `state.session_started_at` but not integrated into cooldown hash or reset logic\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `reminders.py:179-186` - `session_age_minutes` extracted from state in `_build_legacy_context()`\\n  - `reminder_engine.py:54` - `ReminderContext` includes `session_age_minutes` field\\n  - No usage of `session_age_minutes` in cooldown reset or hash generation logic\\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 7: Cross-Project Search Revealed No Prior Work\\n- **Summary:** No existing implementations of session-aware cooldown caching exist across all Scribe projects - this redesign will be pioneering work\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='session cooldown cache isolation')` - No results\\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='reminder deduplication session-aware')` - No results\\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (Medium-High - absence of evidence)\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Technical Analysis\\n<!-- ID: technical_analysis -->\\n\\n### Code Patterns Identified\\n\\n**Current Architecture:**\\n```\\n\\u250c\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2510\\n\\u2502 Tool Execution (append_entry, read_file, etc.)             \\u2502\\n\\u2502   \\u2514\\u2500> get_reminders(project, tool_name, state)             \\u2502\\n\\u2502        \\u2514\\u2500> {reminder_key}\\\"    \\u2502\\n\\u2502                            (NO SESSION_ID)                 \\u2502\\n\\u2502   Cooldown Cache: data/reminder_cooldowns.json            \\u2502\\n\\u2502   Session Tracking: execution_context.py (UNUSED)         \\u2502\\n\\u2514\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2518\\n```\\n\\n**Hash Generation Pattern (Current):**\\n- Format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\\n- Persisted to: `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\\n- Lifetime: 24 hours (configurable via `cooldown_cache_retention_minutes`)\\n- Atomic writes: Uses `.tmp` file then `replace()` for safety\\n\\n**Session Tracking Pattern (Existing but Unused):**\\n- `ExecutionContext.session_id` - Stable UUID persisted across restarts\\n- `ExecutionContext.transport_session_id` - Unstable ID from MCP transport\\n- `RouterContextManager.get_or_create_session_id()` - 3-tier lookup with DB persistence\\n- Tables: `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`\\n\\n### System Interactions\\n\\n**Current Dependencies:**\\n1. **Reminder Engine \\u2192 File System**\\n   - Reads/writes `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\\n   - Atomic write pattern with `.tmp` file\\n   - 24-hour cleanup via `_cleanup_cooldown_cache()`\\n\\n2. **Execution Context \\u2192 Database**\\n   - `upsert_session()` persists session to `scribe_sessions` table\\n   - `get_session_by_transport()` retrieves stable session_id from transport_session_id\\n   - Session heartbeat updates `last_active_at` timestamp\\n\\n3. **Tool Layer \\u2192 Reminder Engine**\\n   - Post-execution call to `get_reminders()`\\n   - No feedback about operation success/failure\\n   - State object passed contains `session_started_at` but no session_id\\n\\n**Missing Integration:**\\n- Reminder engine does NOT query database for session info\\n- Session_id NOT included in cooldown hash keys\\n- No link between `reminder_history` and `agent_sessions` tables\\n\\n### Risk Assessment\\n\\n**Technical Risks:**\\n- \\u2705 **File-Based Race Conditions:** Atomic write pattern (`.tmp` + `replace()`) mitigates concurrent write risks\\n- \\u26a0\\ufe0f **Singleton Shared State:** Global `_reminder_engine` means concurrent sessions share cooldown state (HIGH RISK)\\n- \\u26a0\\ufe0f **Test Data Pollution:** `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` contains test project UUIDs mixed with production data (MEDIUM RISK)\\n- \\u2705 **Database Session Infrastructure:** Existing tables and methods are production-ready (LOW RISK)\\n- \\u26a0\\ufe0f **Migration Complexity:** Moving from file-based to DB-based cooldowns requires careful data migration (MEDIUM RISK)\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Recommendations\\n<!-- ID: recommendations -->\\n\\n### Immediate Next Steps\\n\\n**Architecture Phase:**\\n1. **Design `reminder_history` Table Schema**\\n   - Columns: `id`, `session_id`, `project_root`, `agent_id`, `tool_name`, `reminder_key`, `shown_at`, `context` (success/failure)\\n   - Indexes: `(session_id, reminder_key)`, `(shown_at)` for TTL cleanup\\n   - Foreign key: `session_id` \\u2192 `scribe_sessions.session_id` (ON DELETE CASCADE)\\n\\n2. **Define Session Reset Strategy**\\n   - **Option A: Time-Based (10-minute idle)** - Use `agent_sessions.last_active_at` read_file   format_requested=readable; response_data={\"chunks\": [{\"byte_end\": 11567, \"byte_start\": 0, \"chunk_index\": 0, \"content\": \"\\n# \\ud83d\\udd2c Research: Reminder System Architecture Analysis \\u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\\n**Author:** ResearchAgent\\n**Version:** v1.0\\n**Status:** Complete\\n**Last Updated:** 2026-01-03 11:44:30 UTC\\n\\n> This document provides comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system architecture to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign. It examines current file-based cooldown mechanisms, session tracking capabilities, database infrastructure readiness, and tool integration patterns to identify gaps and design opportunities for session-isolated reminder deduplication.\\n\\n---\\n## Executive Summary\\n<!-- ID: executive_summary -->\\n**Primary Objective:** Conduct comprehensive analysis of the current reminder system to inform DB-integrated, session-aware redesign that enables proper session isolation and failure-triggered reminder prioritization.\\n\\n**Key Takeaways:**\\n- **Critical Gap Identified:** Current file-based cooldown cache (`data/reminder_cooldowns.json`) lacks session isolation - reminders persist across all sessions for the same project+agent+tool combination\\n- **Infrastructure Ready:** Robust session tracking already exists in DB (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables) with session_id support in `execution_context.py`\\n- **Novel Enhancement:** No prior session-aware caching work exists across Scribe projects - this will be pioneering implementation\\n- **Missing Table:** `reminder_history` table needs to be added to SQLite schema for DB-integrated cooldown tracking\\n- **Tool Integration Gap:** No distinction between success/failure context when generating reminders - all reminders treated equally\\n- **Singleton Pattern:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance shared across all sessions prevents per-session state management\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Research Scope\\n<!-- ID: research_scope -->\\n**Research Lead:** ResearchAgent\\n\\n**Investigation Window:** 2026-01-03 11:40 \\u2014 2026-01-03 11:44 UTC\\n\\n**Focus Areas:**\\n- \\u2705 Current reminder architecture (`utils/reminder_engine.py`)\\n- \\u2705 Cooldown cache persistence mechanism (file-based JSON)\\n- \\u2705 Session tracking infrastructure (`shared/execution_context.py`)\\n- \\u2705 Database schema analysis (`storage/sqlite.py`)\\n- \\u2705 Tool integration patterns (append_entry, read_file)\\n- \\u2705 Cross-project learning (session-aware caching patterns)\\n\\n**Dependencies & Constraints:**\\n- **Existing Infrastructure:** Must integrate with existing session tracking (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions` tables)\\n- **Backwards Compatibility:** File-based cooldown cache currently contains 47KB of data from test runs and production usage\\n- **Singleton Architecture:** Current global singleton pattern conflicts with per-session state requirements\\n- **10-Minute Context Window:** Design must support detection and reset of 10-minute context boundaries\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Findings\\n<!-- ID: findings -->\\n\\n### Finding 1: File-Based Cooldown Mechanism Lacks Session Isolation\\n- **Summary:** Current implementation uses composite hash keys (`project_root agent_id tool_name reminder_key`) that persist across all sessions, preventing proper session-aware cooldown isolation\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:282-287` - `_get_reminder_hash()` constructs keys without session_id component\\n  - `utils/reminder_engine.py:236-255` - `_save_cooldown_cache()` persists to `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` with atomic write pattern\\n  - `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` - Real-world file contains 47KB of entries with format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}: {timestamp}`\\n- **Confidence:** 0.97 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 2: Robust Session Tracking Infrastructure Already Exists\\n- **Summary:** Database contains comprehensive session tracking tables (`agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`) with TTL, identity_key, and transport_session_id mapping capabilities\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:681-692` - `agent_sessions` table schema with identity_key, TTL, expires_at fields\\n  - `storage/sqlite.py:740-748` - `scribe_sessions` table with transport_session_id \\u2192 stable session_id mapping\\n  - `shared/execution_context.py:62-112` - `get_or_create_session_id()` implements 3-tier lookup: memory cache \\u2192 DB \\u2192 create new\\n- **Confidence:** 0.96 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 3: Missing reminder_history Table in Database\\n- **Summary:** No `reminder_history` table exists in current SQLite schema - all reminder cooldown tracking is file-based only\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - Searched `storage/sqlite.py` for `CREATE TABLE` statements - found `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`, `agent_projects`, but no reminder-related tables\\n  - Grep for `reminder` in CREATE TABLE context returned no matches\\n- **Confidence:** 1.0 (Absolute)\\n\\n### Finding 4: Singleton Pattern Prevents Per-Session State\\n- **Summary:** Global `_reminder_engine` instance in `reminders.py` is shared across all sessions, preventing session-specific cooldown state management\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `reminders.py:20-28` - `_get_engine()` returns global singleton instance\\n  - `reminder_engine.py:66-87` - `ReminderEngine.__init__()` initializes single `ReminderHistory` object for all sessions\\n- **Confidence:** 0.93 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 5: No Success/Failure Context in Reminder Generation\\n- **Summary:** Tools call `get_reminders()` after main execution completes, but no context is passed about operation outcome (success vs failure) to enable failure-triggered priority reminders\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `tools/append_entry.py:1210-1212` - `get_reminders()` called after entry writing completes\\n  - `tools/read_file.py:502-517` - Reminders generated regardless of read operation outcome\\n  - No status/success parameter passed to `get_reminders()` function\\n- **Confidence:** 0.91 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 6: Session Age Tracking Exists But Is Unused\\n- **Summary:** Session age is calculated from `state.session_started_at` but not integrated into cooldown hash or reset logic\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `reminders.py:179-186` - `session_age_minutes` extracted from state in `_build_legacy_context()`\\n  - `reminder_engine.py:54` - `ReminderContext` includes `session_age_minutes` field\\n  - No usage of `session_age_minutes` in cooldown reset or hash generation logic\\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (High)\\n\\n### Finding 7: Cross-Project Search Revealed No Prior Work\\n- **Summary:** No existing implementations of session-aware cooldown caching exist across all Scribe projects - this redesign will be pioneering work\\n- **Evidence:**\\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='session cooldown cache isolation')` - No results\\n  - `query_entries(search_scope='all_projects', message='reminder deduplication session-aware')` - No results\\n- **Confidence:** 0.88 (Medium-High - absence of evidence)\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Technical Analysis\\n<!-- ID: technical_analysis -->\\n\\n### Code Patterns Identified\\n\\n**Current Architecture:**\\n```\\n\\u250c\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2510\\n\\u2502 Tool Execution (append_entry, read_file, etc.)             \\u2502\\n\\u2502   \\u2514\\u2500> get_reminders(project, tool_name, state)             \\u2502\\n\\u2502        \\u2514\\u2500> _build_legacy_context()                         \\u2502\\n\\u2502             \\u2514\\u2500> ReminderEngine.generate_reminders()        \\u2502\\n\\u2502                  \\u2514\\u2500> _get_reminder_hash()                  \\u2502\\n\\u2502                       \\u2514\\u2500> f\\\"{project_root} {agent_id}      \\u2502\\n\\u2502                             {tool_name} {reminder_key}\\\"    \\u2502\\n\\u2502                            (NO SESSION_ID)                 \\u2502\\n\\u2502   Cooldown Cache: data/reminder_cooldowns.json            \\u2502\\n\\u2502   Session Tracking: execution_context.py (UNUSED)         \\u2502\\n\\u2514\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2518\\n```\\n\\n**Hash Generation Pattern (Current):**\\n- Format: `{project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\\n- Persisted to: `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\\n- Lifetime: 24 hours (configurable via `cooldown_cache_retention_minutes`)\\n- Atomic writes: Uses `.tmp` file then `replace()` for safety\\n\\n**Session Tracking Pattern (Existing but Unused):**\\n- `ExecutionContext.session_id` - Stable UUID persisted across restarts\\n- `ExecutionContext.transport_session_id` - Unstable ID from MCP transport\\n- `RouterContextManager.get_or_create_session_id()` - 3-tier lookup with DB persistence\\n- Tables: `agent_sessions`, `scribe_sessions`, `session_projects`\\n\\n### System Interactions\\n\\n**Current Dependencies:**\\n1. **Reminder Engine \\u2192 File System**\\n   - Reads/writes `data/reminder_cooldowns.json`\\n   - Atomic write pattern with `.tmp` file\\n   - 24-hour cleanup via `_cleanup_cooldown_cache()`\\n\\n2. **Execution Context \\u2192 Database**\\n   - `upsert_session()` persists session to `scribe_sessions` table\\n   - `get_session_by_transport()` retrieves stable session_id from transport_session_id\\n   - Session heartbeat updates `last_active_at` timestamp\\n\\n3. **Tool Layer \\u2192 Reminder Engine**\\n   - Post-execution call to `get_reminders()`\\n   - No feedback about operation success/failure\\n   - State object passed contains `session_started_at` but no session_id\\n\\n**Missing Integration:**\\n- Reminder engine does NOT query database for session info\\n- Session_id NOT included in cooldown hash keys\\n- No link between `reminder_history` and `agent_sessions` tables\\n\\n### Risk Assessment\\n\\n**Technical Risks:**\\n- \\u2705 **File-Based Race Conditions:** Atomic write pattern (`.tmp` + `replace()`) mitigates concurrent write risks\\n- \\u26a0\\ufe0f **Singleton Shared State:** Global `_reminder_engine` means concurrent sessions share cooldown state (HIGH RISK)\\n- \\u26a0\\ufe0f **Test Data Pollution:** `data/reminder_cooldowns.json` contains test project UUIDs mixed with production data (MEDIUM RISK)\\n- \\u2705 **Database Session Infrastructure:** Existing tables and methods are production-ready (LOW RISK)\\n- \\u26a0\\ufe0f **Migration Complexity:** Moving from file-based to DB-based cooldowns requires careful data migration (MEDIUM RISK)\\n\\n\\n---\\n## Recommendations\\n<!-- ID: recommendations -->\\n\\n### Immediate Next Steps\\n\\n**Architecture Phase:**\\n1. **Design `reminder_history` Table Schema**\\n   - Columns: `id`, `session_id`, `project_root`, `agent_id`, `tool_name`, `reminder_key`, `shown_at`, `context` (success/failure)\\n   - Indexes: `(session_id, reminder_key)`, `(shown_at)` for TTL cleanup\\n   - Foreign key: `session_id` \\u2192 `scribe_sessions.session_id` (ON DELETE CASCADE)\\n\\n2. **Define Session Reset Strategy**\\n   - **Option A: Time-Based (10-minute idle)** - Use `agent_sessions.last_active_at` to detect context boundaries\\n   - **Option B: Context-Based** - Explicit session end events trigger cooldown reset\\n   - **Recommendation:** Hybrid approach - reset on 10-minute idle OR explicit session end\\n\\n3. **Specify Failure-Triggered Reminder Priority**\\n   - Add `operation_status` parameter to `get_reminders()` function\\n   - Prioritize failure-context reminders (e.g., \\\"no logs\\\" after failed `append_entry`)\\n   - Lower priority for success-context reminders\\n\\n**Implementation Phase:**\\n4. **Refactor Hash Generation**\\n   - Include `session_id` in cooldown hash: `{session_id} {project_root} {agent_id} {tool_name} {reminder_key}`\\n   - Update `_get_reminder_hash()` to accept `session_id` parameter\\n\\n5. **Create DB Storage Methods**\\n   - `upsert_reminder_shown(session_id, reminder_key, context)` - Record reminder display\\n   - `check_reminder_cooldown(session_id, reminder_key, cooldown_minutes)` - Query recent displays\\n\", Problem Statement\\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\\n## 1. Problem Statement\\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\\n- **Context:** Scribe MCP tools return JSON-wrapped outputs that are difficult for AI agents to read and use effectively. The `read_file` tool exemplifies this: content is buried in nested structures with escaped newlines, no line numbers, and metadata mixed with content. Agents avoid using these tools despite their audit capabilities.\\n- **Goals:**\\n  - Make ALL Scribe MCP tool outputs agent-friendly and highly readable\\n  - Preserve full audit trail (sha256, provenance, timestamps, agent identity)\\n  - Start with `read_file` as the priority tool, then expand to all tools\\n  - Create output formats that agents WANT to use\\n  - Maintain backward compatibility for existing integrations\\n- **Non-Goals:**\\n  - Breaking existing API contracts\\n  - Removing audit/provenance capabilities\\n  - Creating entirely new tools (we refine existing ones)\\n- **Success Metrics:**\\n  - Agents prefer Scribe tools over raw file operations\\n  - Output is readable at a glance (line numbers, actual line breaks)\\n  - Audit data is accessible but not obstructive\\n  - All existing tests continue to pass\\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\\n## 2. Requirements & Constraints\\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\\n\\n### Functional Requirements\\n\", \"line_end\": 50, \"line_start\": 26}, \"frontmatter\": {\"category\": \"engineering\", "{\"chunk\": {\"byte_end\": 4061, \"byte_start\": 0, \"content\": \"---\\nid: scribe_tool_output_refinement-architecture\\ntitle: \\\"\\\\U0001F3D7\\\\uFE0F Architecture Guide \\\\u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\\\"\\ndoc_type: architecture\\ncategory: engineering\\nstatus: draft\\nversion: '0.1'\\nlast_updated: '2026-01-02'\\nmaintained_by: Corta Labs\\ncreated_by: Corta Labs\\nowners: []\\nrelated_docs: []\\ntags: []\\nsummary: ''\\n---\\n\\n# \\ud83c\\udfd7\\ufe0f Architecture Guide \\u2014 scribe_tool_output_refinement\\n**Author:** Scribe\\n**Version:** Draft v0.1\\n**Status:** Draft\\n**Last Updated:** 2026-01-02 13:47:01 UTC\\n\\n> Architecture guide for scribe_tool_output_refinement.\\n\\n---\\n## 1. Problem Statement\\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\\n## 1. Problem Statement\\n<!-- ID: problem_statement -->\\n- **Context:** Scribe MCP tools return JSON-wrapped outputs that are difficult for AI agents to read and use effectively. The `read_file` tool exemplifies this: content is buried in nested structures with escaped newlines, no line numbers, and metadata mixed with content. Agents avoid using these tools despite their audit capabilities.\\n- **Goals:**\\n  - Make ALL Scribe MCP tool outputs agent-friendly and highly readable\\n  - Preserve full audit trail (sha256, provenance, timestamps, agent identity)\\n  - Start with `read_file` as the priority tool, then expand to all tools\\n  - Create output formats that agents WANT to use\\n  - Maintain backward compatibility for existing integrations\\n- **Non-Goals:**\\n  - Breaking existing API contracts\\n  - Removing audit/provenance capabilities\\n  - Creating entirely new tools (we refine existing ones)\\n- **Success Metrics:**\\n  - Agents prefer Scribe tools over raw file operations\\n  - Output is readable at a glance (line numbers, actual line breaks)\\n  - Audit data is accessible but not obstructive\\n  - All existing tests continue to pass\\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\\n## 2. Requirements & Constraints\\n<!-- ID: requirements_constraints -->\\n\\n### Functional Requirements\\n\\n**FR1: Multi-Format Output Support**\\n- All tools MUST support three output formats: `readable`, `structured`, `compact`\\n- Format selection via optional `format` parameter (default: `structured` for backward compatibility)\\n- Each format serves distinct use case:\\n  - `readable`: Agent-friendly with line numbers, visual separators, actual line breaks\\n  - `structured`: Current JSON format for programmatic consumers (backward compatible)\\n  - `compact`: Minimal output for token-constrained scenarios\\n\\n**FR2: Readable Format Specification**\\n- Content MUST be line-numbered using cat -n style: `  1\\u2192content`, `  2\\u2192content`\\n- Line numbers right-aligned with arrow separator for scannability\\n- Actual line breaks (NOT escaped `\\\\n`)\\n- Metadata in separate collapsible section with visual boundaries\\n- ASCII art separators for clear visual hierarchy\\n\\n**FR3: Priority Tool Coverage**\\n1. `read_file` - File content with provenance (HIGHEST PRIORITY)\\n2. `append_entry` - Log confirmation\\n3. `read_recent` - Recent log entries\\n4. `query_entries` - Search results\\n5. `list_projects` - Project listing\\n6. All remaining 9 tools\\n\\n**FR4: Metadata Preservation**\\n- All audit data MUST be preserved: sha256, timestamps, provenance, agent identity\\n- Metadata placed in separate section (not mixed with content)\\n- Collapsible metadata blocks using ASCII separators\\n- Full audit trail accessible in all formats\\n\\n### Non-Functional Requirements\\n\\n**NFR1: Backward Compatibility**\\n- Default format MUST be `structured` (current behavior)\\n- Existing tool responses MUST remain unchanged when format parameter not specified\\n- All existing tests MUST pass without modification\\n- No breaking changes to tool APIs\\n\\n**NFR2: Performance**\\n- Output formatting MUST NOT degrade performance below existing baselines\\n- `readable` format generation \\u2264 5ms overhead per tool call\\n- Memory overhead \\u2264 2x current usage (for formatted strings)\\n- Existing performance test suite MUST pass\\n\\n**NFR3: Maintainability**\\n- Extend existing `utils/response.py` - NO new modules\\n- Single source of truth for formatting logic\\n- Consistent API across all tools\\n- Clear documentation for format implementations\\n\\n\", \"line_end\": 100, \"line_start\": 1},
